Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions and Reducibility of scalar type generalized Verma modules for classical Lie algebras Thanks: *Corresponding author
Zhanqiang Bai and Jing Jiang*

Abstract
. Let 𝔤 \mathfrak{g} be a classial Lie algebra and 𝔭 \mathfrak{p} be a maximal parabolic subalgebra. Let M M be a generalized Verma module induced from a one dimensional representation of 𝔭 \mathfrak{p} . Such M M is called a scalar type generalized Verma module. Its simple quotient L L is a highest weight moudle. In this paper, we will determine the reducibility of such scalar type generalized Verma modules by computing the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of L L . Keywords: Generalized Verma module; Gelfand-Kirillov dimension; Young tableau



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
17B10, 17B20, 22E47

1.
Introduction

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a finite-dimensional complex semisimple Lie
algebra and
U(\mathfrak{g})
be its universal enveloping algebra.
Fix a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
and denote by
\Delta
the root system associated with
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h})
. Choose a positive root system

\Delta^{+}\subset\Delta
and a simple system
\Pi\subset\Delta^{+}
. Let
\rho
be the half sum of roots in
\Delta^{+}
. Let

\mathfrak{g}=\bar{\mathfrak{u}}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{u}
be the Cartan decomposition of
\mathfrak{g}
with nilpotent radical
\mathfrak{u}
and its dual space
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}
. Choose a subset
I\subset\Pi
and it generates a subsystem

\Delta_{I}\subset\Delta
.
Let
\mathfrak{q}_{I}
be the standard parabolic subalgebra corresponding to
I
with Levi decomposition
\mathfrak{q}_{I}=\mathfrak{l}_{I}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{I}
. We frequently drop the
subscript
I
if there is no confusion.

Let
F(\lambda)
be a finite-dimensional irreducible
\mathfrak{l}
-module with highest weight
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. It can also be viewed as a

\mathfrak{q}
-module with trivial
\mathfrak{u}
-action. The
generalized Verma modules

M_{I}(\lambda)
is defined by

M_{I}(\lambda):=U(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{q})}F(\lambda).

The simple quotient of
M_{I}(\lambda)
is denoted by
L(\lambda)
. In the case when
\dim(F(\lambda))=1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is called a generalized Verma
module of
scalar type
.

The reducibility of generalized Verma modules is very importan in representation theory. It is closely related with many other problems, see for example
[
3
,
9
,
17
]
. The most important tool is Jantzen’s criterion
[
12
]
.
But in general, this criterion is very complicated in application. Kubo
[
16
]
found some practical reducibility criteria for all the scalar generalized Verma modules associated with maximal parabolic subalgebras. By using Kubo’s result, He
[
10
]
gave the reducibily for all scalar-type generalized Verma modules of Hermitian symmetric pairs. Then He-Kubo-Zierau
[
11
]
found the reducibily for all scalar-type generalized Verma modules associated with maximal parabolic subalgebras. Bai-Xiao
[
3
]
found the reducibily for all generalized Verma modules of Hermitian symmetric pairs.

Gelfand-Kirillov dimension
is an important invariant for infinite-dimensional algebraic structures. Since Joseph
[
13
]
, Gelfand-Kirillov dimension is used to measure the size of representations of Lie algebras and Lie groups.
Recently Bai-Xiao
[
2
]
proved that a scalar-type generalized Verma module
M
is reducible if and only if the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of its simple quotient
L
is strictly less than
\dim(\mathfrak{u}_{I})
. By using this result and their algorithms
[
4
]
of computing GK dimensions, we can give a new proof for
the complete list of parameters
\lambda+\rho
for which the scalar-type generalized Verma modules associated with maximal parabolic subalgebras are reducible. Then by using same method, we can give the GK dimensions of all scalar type highest weight moudles. Those modules with minimal positive GK dimensions can be easily found from our formula.

The paper is organized as follows. The necessary priliminaries for maximal parabolic subalgebras and Gelfand-Kirillov dimension are given in Section
2
and
3
. In Section
4
, we give the reducibility of scalar generalized Verma modules for type
A
. In Section
5
, we give the reducibility of scalar generalized Verma modules for types
B
and
C
. In Section
6
, we give the reducibility of scalar generalized Verma modules for type
D
. In Section
7
, we give the explicit formula of Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions for scalar type highest weight modules.

Acknowledgments.

This project is supported by the National Science Foundation of China (Grant No. 12171344).

2.
Maximal parabolic subalgebras

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a finite-dimensional complex semisimple Lie
algebra and let
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{\alpha\in\Delta^{+}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}

be a fixed borel subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. For a maximal parabolic subalgebra
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{u}
, it corresponds to the subsets
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{i}\}

for some
\alpha_{i}\in\Pi
.
In this section, we give the explicit values of
\dim(\mathfrak{u})
. The results are as follows.

(1)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
A_{n-1}
, for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{p}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}-e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq p\quad\text{or}\quad p+1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{u})=\{e_{i}-e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq p,\>p+1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

So
\dim(\mathfrak{u})=p(n-p)
.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
B_{n}
,

(a)

for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{1}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}\pm e_{j}|2\leq i<j\leq n\}\cup\{e_{j}|2\leq j\leq n\},

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{u})=\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{1}\pm e_{j}|1\leq j\leq n\}\cup\{e_{1}\}.

So
\dim(\mathfrak{u})=2(n-1)+1=2n-1
.

(b)

for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{p}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})

\displaystyle=\{e_{i}-e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq p\}\cup\{e_{i}\pm e_{j}|p+1\leq i<j\leq n\}

\displaystyle\cup\{e_{j}|p+1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

So

\displaystyle\dim(\mathfrak{u})

\displaystyle=|\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})|

\displaystyle=n^{2}-C^{2}_{p}-(n-p)-2C^{2}_{n-p}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

(c)

for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{n}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}-e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq n\},

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{u})=\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}+e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq n\}\cup\{e_{j}|1\leq j\leq n\}.

So
\dim(\mathfrak{u})=C^{2}_{n}+n=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}
.

(3)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
C_{n}
, in the case of
B_{n}
and
C_{n}
,
\dim(\mathfrak{u})
is equal.

(4)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
D_{n}

(a)

for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{1}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}\pm e_{j}|2\leq i<j\leq n\},

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{u})=\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{1}\pm e_{j}|2\leq i<j\leq n\}.

So
\dim(\mathfrak{u})=2(n-1)
.

(b)

for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{p}\}
, we have

\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}-e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq p\}\cup\{e_{i}\pm e_{j}|p+1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

So
\dim(\mathfrak{u})=|\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})|=n^{2}-n-C^{2}_{p}-2C^{2}_{n-p}=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p
.

(c)

for
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{n}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}-e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq n\},

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{u})=\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{e_{i}+e_{j}|1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

So
\dim(\mathfrak{u})=C^{2}_{n}=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
.

For scalar generalized Verma modules associated with a maximal parabolic subalgebra, we have the following two lemmas.

Lemma 2.1

(
[
11
, Lemma 1.4]
)
.

When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type, we will have
\lambda=z\xi_{i}
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
and
\xi_{i}
is the fundamental weight corresponding to the simple root
\alpha_{i}
associated with the given maximal parabolic subalgebra.

Lemma 2.2

(
[
2
, Lemma 4.5]
)
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathrm{GKdim}(L((z+1)\xi))\leq\mathrm{GKdim}(L(z\xi)).

In particular, if
M_{I}(z\xi)
is reducible, then
M_{I}((z+1)\xi)
is also reducible.

3.
Gelfand-Kirillov dimension

Let
M
be a finite generated
U(\mathfrak{g})
-module. Fix a finite dimensional generating subspace
M_{0}
of
M
. Let
U_{n}(\mathfrak{g})
be the standard filtration of
U(\mathfrak{g})
. Set
M_{n}=U_{n}(\mathfrak{g})\cdot M_{0}
and

\text{gr}(M)=\bigoplus\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\text{gr}_{n}M,

where
\text{gr}_{n}M=M_{n}/{M_{n-1}}
. Thus
\text{gr}(M)
is a graded module of
\text{gr}(U(\mathfrak{g}))\simeq S(\mathfrak{g})
.

Definition 3.1
.

The
Gelfand-Kirillov dimension
of
M
is defined by

\operatorname{GKdim}M=\varlimsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\dim(U_{n}(\mathfrak{g})M_{0})}{\log n}.

Definition 3.2
.

The
associated variety
of
M
is defined by

V(M):=\{X\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\mid f(X)=0\text{ for all~}f\in\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\operatorname{gr}M)\}.

The above two definitions are independent of the choice of
M_{0}
, and
\dim V(M)=\operatorname{GKdim}M
(e.g.,
[
18
]
).

Definition 3.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a finite-dimensional semisimple Lie algebra. Let
I
be a two-sided idel in
U(\mathfrak{g})
. Then
\text{gr}(U(\mathfrak{g})/I)\simeq S(\mathfrak{g})/\text{gr}I
is a graded
S(\mathfrak{g})
-module. Its annihilator is
\text{gr}I
. We define its associated variety by

V(I):=V(U(\mathfrak{g})/I)=\{X\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\mid p(X)=0\ \mbox{for all $p\in{\text{gr}}I$}\}.

We have the following theorem.

Theorem 3.4

(
[
14
]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a reductive Lie algebra and
I
be a primitive ideal in
U(\mathfrak{g})
.Then
V(I)
is the closure of a single nilpotent coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}_{I}
in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. In particular, for a highest weight module
L(\lambda)
, we have
V(AnnL(\lambda))=\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{Ann(L(\lambda))}
.

Let
G
be a connected semisimple finite dimensional complex algebraic group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
W
be the Weyl group of
\mathfrak{g}
. We use
L_{w}
to denote the simple highest weight
\mathfrak{g}
-module of highest weight
-w\rho-\rho
with
w\in W
. We denote
I_{w}=\mathrm{Ann}(L_{w})
. Then we have the following theorem.

Theorem 3.5

(
[
6
]
)
.

V(I_{w})=\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{w}
.

From Vogan
[
19
]
, we have
\dim V(I_{w})=2\mathrm{GKdim}(L_{w})
.
In
[
5
]
and
[
4
]
, Bai-Xiao-Xie have found an algorithm to compute the Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions of highest weight modules of classical Lie algebras. We recall their algorithms here.

For a totally ordered set
\Gamma
, we denote by
\mathrm{Seq}_{n}(\Gamma)
the set of sequences
x=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})
of length
n
with
x_{i}\in\Gamma
.
We say
q=(q_{1},\cdots,q_{N})
is the
dual partition
of a partition
p=(p_{1},\cdots,p_{N})
and write
q=p^{t}
if
q_{i}
is the length of
i
-th column of the Young diagram
p
.
Let
p(x)
be the shape of the Young tableau
Y(x)
obtained by applying Robinson-Schensted algorithm to
x\in\mathrm{Seq}_{n}(\Gamma)
. For convenience, we set
q(x)=p(x)^{t}
.

For a Young diagram
p
, use
(k,l)
to denote the box in the
k
-th row and the
l
-th column.
We say the box
(k,l)
is
even
(resp.
odd
) if
k+l
is even (resp. odd). Let
p_{i}^{ev}
(resp.
p_{i}^{odd}
) be the number of even (resp. odd) boxes in the
i
-th row of the Young diagram
p
.
One can easily check that

(3.6)

p_{i}^{ev}=\begin{cases}\left\lceil\frac{p_{i}}{2}\right\rceil&\text{ if }i\text{ is odd},\\
\left\lfloor\frac{p_{i}}{2}\right\rfloor&\text{ if }i\text{ is even},\end{cases}\quad p_{i}^{odd}=\begin{cases}\left\lfloor\frac{p_{i}}{2}\right\rfloor&\text{ if }i\text{ is odd},\\
\left\lceil\frac{p_{i}}{2}\right\rceil&\text{ if }i\text{ is even}.\end{cases}

Here for
a\in\mathbb{R}
,
\lfloor a\rfloor
is the largest integer
n
such that
n\leq a
, and
\lceil a\rceil
is the smallest integer
n
such that
n\geq a
. For convenience, we set

p^{ev}=(p_{1}^{ev},p_{2}^{ev},\cdots)\quad\mbox{and}\quad p^{odd}=(p_{1}^{odd},p_{2}^{odd},\cdots).

For
x=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathrm{Seq}_{n}(\Gamma)
, set

\displaystyle{x}^{-}=

\displaystyle(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n-1},x_{n},-x_{n},-x_{n-1},\cdots,-x_{2},-x_{1}).

Theorem 3.7

(
[
4
, Theorem 1.5]
)
.

Let
\lambda+\rho=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{n})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
be integral. Then

\emph{GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p(\lambda+\rho)_{i}&if\>\Delta=A_{n-1}\\
n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}&if\>\Delta=B_{n}/C_{n}\\
n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}&if\>\Delta=D_{n}\end{array}\right.

We define three functions
F_{A}
,
F_{B}
,
F_{D}
as

\displaystyle F_{A}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}(k-1)p_{k},

\displaystyle F_{B}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}(k-1)p_{k}^{ev},

\displaystyle F_{D}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}(k-1)p_{k}^{odd},

where
p=p(x)=(p_{1},p_{2},\cdots)
.

Definition 3.8
.

Fix
\lambda+\rho=(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{n})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathbb{C})
, we define
[\lambda]
to be the set of maximal subsequences
x
of
\lambda+\rho
such that any two entries of
x
has an integral difference.

For
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}(n,\mathbb{C}),\mathfrak{so}(2n+1,\mathbb{C})
or
\mathfrak{so}(2n,\mathbb{C})
, we define
[\lambda]
to be the set of maximal subsequences
x
of
\lambda+\rho
such that any two entries of
x
have an integral difference or sum. In this case, we set
[\lambda]_{1}
(resp.
[\lambda]_{2}
) be to the subset of
[\lambda]
consisting of sequences with all entries belonging to
\mathbb{Z}
(resp.
\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
).
Since there is at most one element in
[\lambda]_{1}
and
[\lambda]_{2}
, we denote them by
(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}
and
(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}
.
We set
[\lambda]_{1,2}=[\lambda]_{1}\cup[\lambda]_{2},\quad[\lambda]_{3}=[\lambda]\setminus[\lambda]_{1,2}
.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
x=(\lambda_{i_{1}},\lambda_{i_{2}},\cdots\lambda_{i_{r}})\in[\lambda]_{3}
. Let
y=(\lambda_{j_{1}},\lambda_{j_{2}},\cdots,\lambda_{j_{p}})
be the maximal subsequence of
x
such that
j_{1}=i_{1}
and the difference of any two entries of
y
is an integer. Let
z=(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\cdots,\lambda_{k_{q}})
be the subsequence obtained by deleting
y
from
x
, which is possible empty.
Define

\tilde{x}=(\lambda_{j_{1}},\lambda_{j_{2}},\cdots,\lambda_{j_{p}},-\lambda_{k_{q}},-\lambda_{k_{q-1}},\cdots,-\lambda_{k_{1}}).

Theorem 3.10

(
[
4
, Theorem 5.8]
)
.

The GK dimension of
L(\lambda)
can be computed as follows.

(1)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathbb{C})
,

\operatorname{GKdim}L(\lambda)=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}-\sum_{x\in[\lambda]}F_{A}(x).

(2)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}(n,\mathbb{C})
,

\operatorname{GKdim}L(\lambda)=n^{2}-F_{B}((\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-})-F_{D}((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})-\sum_{x\in[\lambda]_{3}}F_{A}(\tilde{x}).

(3)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n+1,\mathbb{C})
,

\operatorname{GKdim}L(\lambda)=n^{2}-F_{B}((\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-})-F_{B}((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})-\sum_{x\in[\lambda]_{3}}F_{A}(\tilde{x}).

(4)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n,\mathbb{C})
,

\operatorname{GKdim}L(\lambda)=n^{2}-n-F_{D}((\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-})-F_{D}((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})-\sum_{x\in[\lambda]_{3}}F_{A}(\tilde{x}).

The follow lemma is very useful in our proof.

Lemma 3.11

(
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
)
.

A scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible if and only if
\emph{GKdim}\>(L(\lambda))=\dim(\mathfrak{u})
.

4.
Reducibility of scalar generalized Verma modules for type
A_{n}

From Lemma
2.1
and Lemma
2.2
, we only need to find the first reducible point of the scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(z\xi)
.

First we recall some notation and results from Bai-Xie
[
5
]
.

Definition 4.1
.

We say
\lambda+\rho\in h^{\ast}
is
(p,q)
-dominant if
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
for all
i,j
such that
1\leq i<j\leq p
or
p+1\leq i<j\leq p+q
, where
\lambda+\rho=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n})
. In particular,
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}>\dots>\lambda_{p}
and
\lambda_{p+1}>\lambda_{p+2}>\dots>\lambda_{p+q}
.

Lemma 4.2

(
[
2
, Theorem 5.2]
)
.

Assume that
\lambda\in h^{\ast}
is
(p,q)
-dominant.

(1)

If
\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{p+1}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
that is,
\lambda
is an integral weight, then
P(\lambda)
is a Young tableau
with at most two columns. And in this case
\emph{GKdim}\>(L(\lambda))=m(n-m)
where m is the number of entries in the second column of P(
\lambda
).

(2)

If
\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{p+1}\notin\mathbb{Z}
,
then
P(\lambda)
consists of two Young tableaux with single column, and in this case
\emph{GKdim}\>(L(\lambda))=pq
.

Remark 4.3
.

In this paper, the element
i
in a Young tableau represents the i-th component of
\lambda+\rho

\>or\>(\lambda+\rho)^{-}
. For example, the Young tableau for
\lambda+\rho=(2,0,3,1)
is

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}.

We use

2
4
1
3

to represent our Young tableau
Y
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{C})
.
M_{I}(\lambda)
is a scalar generalized Verma module with highest weight
\lambda=z\xi
, where
\xi=\xi_{p}
is the fundamental weight corresponding to
\alpha_{p}=e_{p}-e_{p+1}
. Then
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in 1-\min\{p,n-p\}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

Take
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(\mathfrak{n},\mathbb{C})
, and
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{p-1},\alpha_{p+1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\}
,
\alpha_{p}=e_{p}-e_{p+1}
.

When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type, we know that
\lambda=z\xi
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.1
,
and
\xi=(\underbrace{a,\dots,a}_{p},\underbrace{a-1,\dots,a-1}_{n-p})
for
a=\frac{n-p}{n}
.

In
[
9
]
, we know that
2\rho=(n-1,n-3,\dots,-n+3,-n+1)
, thus

\rho=(\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{n-3}{2},\dots,\frac{-n+3}{2},\frac{-n+1}{2}),

\lambda+\rho=(\underbrace{za+\frac{n-1}{2}\dots,za+\frac{n-2p+1}{2}}_{p},\underbrace{(a-1)z+\frac{n-2p-1}{2},\dots,(a-1)z+\frac{-n+1}{2}}_{n-p}).

When
za+\frac{n-2p+1}{2}-(a-1)z\frac{n-2p-1}{2}=z+1\notin\mathbb{Z}
, we will have
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
, and

\displaystyle\rm{GKdim}\>(L(\lambda))

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}-\frac{(n-p)(n-p-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{p^{2}}{2}+np-\frac{p^{2}}{2}=np-p^{2}=p(n-p)=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we obtain that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

When
za+\frac{n-2p+1}{2}-(a-1)z\frac{n-2p-1}{2}=z+1\in\mathbb{Z}
, in other words,
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,

z\xi+\rho=\lambda+\rho
is
(p,q)
-dominant. By Lemma
4.2
, we know that
{\rm GKdim}\>(L(\lambda))=m(n-m)
, where
m
is the number of entries in the second column of
P(\lambda)
.

When
m=\min\{p,n-p\}
,
{\rm GKdim}\>(L(\lambda))=m(n-m)=p(n-p)=\dim(\mathfrak{u})
, in this case
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

When
m(n-m)<p(n-p)=\dim(\mathfrak{u})\Rightarrow(m-p)n<(m+p)(m-p)
,
in this case
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible. In other words, when
m<\min\{p,n-p\}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible. Suppose that
p\leq n-p
.

(1)

When
z=-1
, we will have

m=(2p-1-(p+1)+1)=p-1<p
, in this case
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(4)

When
z=-p
, we will have

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-97.63916pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

Thus
m=(2p-(p+1)+1)=p
, in this case
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So,
z=1-p
is the first reducible point. Similarly if
p\geq n-p
,
z=p-n+1
is the first reducible point.
Hence for
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{C})
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in 1-\min\{p,n-p\}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
∎

Example 4.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(3,\mathbb{C})
, We consider the parabolic subalgebra
\mathfrak{q}
corresponding to the subset
\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{1}\}
.
In this case, we have
p=1
and
n=3
. So the scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(\lambda)
(with highest weight
\lambda=z\xi_{1}
) is reducible if and only if

z\in 1-\min\{1,2\}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}=\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. This result is the same with
[
10
]
and
[
16
]
.

5.
Reducibility of scalar genralized Verma modules for types
B_{n}
and
C_{n}

In this section, we consider the types
B_{n}
and
C_{n}
. Let
M_{I}(\lambda)
be a scalar generalized Verma module with highest weight
\lambda=z\xi
, where
\xi=\xi_{p}
is the fundamental weight corresponding to the simple root
\alpha_{p}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n+1,\mathbb{C})
.
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if

(1)

if
p=\mathrm{1}
, then

\quad z\in(\frac{3}{2}-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
;

(2)

if
\mathrm{2}\leq p\leq n-1
, then

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1-n+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $p$ is even or}\>\>3p<2n+1\\
\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $p$ is odd and}\>\>3p\geq 2n+1\end{array}\right.;

(3)

if
p=n
, then

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $n$ is even}\\
1-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $n$ is odd}\\
\end{array}\right..

Proof.

Take
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n+1,\mathbb{C})
, and
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n-1}\}
,
where

\alpha_{i}=e_{i}-e_{i+1}\>\>1\leq i\leq n-1
and
\alpha_{n}=e_{n}
. When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type, we know that
\lambda=z\xi
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.1
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute
that
\xi=(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2})
,
then

\lambda+\rho=(\frac{1}{2}z+n-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}z+n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}z+\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}z+n-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}z+\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}z-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{1}{2}z+\frac{1}{2}-n).

When
n
is even, we will have the follows.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, then

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}
is integral, by Lemma
3.7
, we know that

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}.

When
\frac{1}{2}z+\frac{1}{2}>-\frac{1}{2}z-\frac{1}{2}\Rightarrow z>-1
, we have

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1)}_{2n}\>\>{\rm and}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1)}_{2n}.

Thus,

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+4\cdot 0+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 0+(2n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-1)

(5.2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{2n\cdot n}{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we know that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2,\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1)}_{2n-2}
and

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,0,\dots,1,0)}_{2n-1}
.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-3)\cdot 1+(2n-2)\cdot 0)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=2n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we know that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,0,1,0,1\dots,0,1)}_{2n-2}
, and

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=2n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we know that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

z=2-n
, we will have

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n-2},0,1,0,1)
, and

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+3+\dots+n-3+n-1+n+1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n-2)(n-3)}{2}-2n

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we know that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

z=1-n
, we will have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-97.63916pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -147.64 M 0 -147.64 L 0 -177.17 L 29.53 -177.17 L 29.53 -147.64 Z M 29.53 -177.17 M 14.76 -162.4}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-118.97873pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n})
, and

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{n(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we know that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Thus
z=2-n
is the first reducible point.

(2)

If
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
, then
(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=0
,
(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-}=0
and
\lambda+\rho\in[\lambda]_{3}
.

From Theorem
3.10
we will have

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-F_{B}((\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-})-F_{B}((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})-\sum_{x\in[\lambda]_{3}}F_{A}(\tilde{x})

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p_{i}(\lambda+\rho)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n)(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Hence if
n
is even,
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible and if
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in 2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

When
n
is odd, we will have the follows.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
, from (
1
) we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u})
.
By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-3)\cdot 1+(2n-2)\cdot 0)

3.11

is reducible.

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,0,1,0,1\dots,0,1)}_{2n-2}.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-1)\cdot 0)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}=n^{2}+2n-1=2n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=1-n
, we will have

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n-1},0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-2)\cdot 1+(n-1)\cdot 0+n\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}=(1+2+\dots+n-2)-n

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}-n

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(d)

When
z=-n
, we will have

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n})
.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n)(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=1-n
is the first reducible point.

(2)

If
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
, we will have similar arguments (i.e., when
n
is even). Thus
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible when
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
.

All in all, when
p=n
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\text{if $n$ is even}\\
1-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\text{if $n$ is odd}.\end{array}\right.

Now we take
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{n-1},\alpha_{n}\}
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute that
\xi=(1,\underbrace{0,0,\dots,0}_{n-1})
.

So we have

\lambda+\rho=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-z+\frac{1}{2}-n).

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, we will have the follows.

(a)

When
z>-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}=(0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1).

By Theorem
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 0+(2n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
we know that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-\frac{3}{2},n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n,\frac{3}{2}-n),

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -29.53 M 29.53 -29.53 L 29.53 -59.06 L 59.06 -59.06 L 59.06 -29.53 Z M 59.06 -59.06 M 44.29 -44.29}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{30.30794pt}{-33.62045pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -59.06 M 0 -59.06 L 0 -88.58 L 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -59.06 Z M 29.53 -88.58 M 14.76 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.66978pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2,2,\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{2n-4})
and
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-4}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-1)^{2}=2n-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Thus
z=0
is the first reducible point.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(0)\>
and
\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-}=(n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n)
.

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in[\lambda]_{3}}F_{A}(\tilde{x})

\displaystyle=0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1

\displaystyle=1+3+\dots+2n-3

\displaystyle=\frac{(2n-2)(n-1)}{2}=(n-1)^{2}.

So we can conclude that

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-0-(n-1)^{2}=2n-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, we will have the follows.

(a)

When
z=-\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-1,n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n,1-n),

(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(n-1,1-n)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-}=(n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n),

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})_{i}^{odd}=(n-1)^{2}.

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1)-(n-1)^{2}=2n-2<\dim(\mathfrak{u})
.

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(1,n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n,-1)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(1,-1).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-2<\dim(\mathfrak{u})
. By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(0,n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n,0)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(0,0).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})
. By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=\frac{3}{2}-n
is the first reducible point.

All in all, when
p=1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\frac{3}{2}-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
.

Finally, we consider the second case.

Take
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{p-1},\alpha_{p+1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\}
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute that
\xi=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{p},0,\cdots,0)
.

So we have

\lambda+\rho=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},\cdots,z+n-p+\frac{1}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\cdots,\frac{1}{2})

First we consider the case when
p
is odd and
3p\geq 2n+1
.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, we will have the follows.

(a)

When
z>-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(0,1,\dots,0,1).

By Theorem
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 0+(2n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,n-p-\frac{1}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\cdots,\frac{1}{2}).

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{2.24858pt}{-33.62045pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{24.25337pt}{-33.18993pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -59.06 M 0 -59.06 L 0 -88.58 L 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -59.06 Z M 29.53 -88.58 M 14.76 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.66978pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

In the above tableau, we denote
k=2n-p
.

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-4}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

(5.3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=2n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n-1},0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+4+\dots+2n-2p-2+(2n-2p-1)\cdot 2)

\displaystyle-(2n-2p+\dots+p-2+p)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-2C^{2}_{n-p}-2(n-p)^{2}-2n+p-\frac{(2n-p-1)(3p-2n-2)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-1.

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(d)

When
z=-\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1),

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p},\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n})
.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+4+\dots+2n-2p-2+(2n-2p-1)\cdot 2)

\displaystyle-(2n-2p+\dots+p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-2C^{2}_{n-p}-2(n-p)^{2}-\frac{(2n-p-1)(3p-2n)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first reducible point.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)_{(z)}=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},\dots,z+n-p+\frac{1}{2})\in[\lambda]_{3}.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-(1+2+\dots+p-1)

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, we will have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
.

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=-\frac{3}{2}
, we will have

(5.4)

\displaystyle(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2}),

(5.5)

\displaystyle(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}=(n-2,\dots,n-p-1,-n+p+1,\dots,-n+2).

We divide the discussion into two cases:

(i)

p=n-1
. We will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p-2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2p-3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2p-3+2p-1)+2p-1

\displaystyle=2np-2p^{2}+2p-1

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\frac{3}{2}p-1

\displaystyle<2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

(ii)

p<n-1
. We will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2p-1)

\displaystyle=2np-2p^{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p

\displaystyle<2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

All in all, when
z=-\frac{3}{2}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{3}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{3}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2}).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{2.6013pt}{-33.13435pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -29.53 M 29.53 -29.53 L 29.53 -59.06 L 59.06 -59.06 L 59.06 -29.53 Z M 59.06 -59.06 M 44.29 -44.29}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{32.00935pt}{-32.00935pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -59.06 M 0 -59.06 L 0 -88.58 L 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -59.06 Z M 29.53 -88.58 M 14.76 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.66978pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -59.06 M 29.53 -59.06 L 29.53 -88.58 L 59.06 -88.58 L 59.06 -59.06 Z M 59.06 -88.58 M 44.29 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{24.25337pt}{-54.5295pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{p-2},1,0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-(1+2+\dots+p-2+p)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-p-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-1.

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(d)

When
z=-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-(1+2+\dots+p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-p)^{2}-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Hence
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first reducible point.

So when
p
is odd and
3p\geq 2n+1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(1-n+\frac{p}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Next we consider the case when
p
is even.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq 0
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u})\Rightarrow M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=-1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible since (
1b
).

(c)

When
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we divide the discussion into three cases:

(i)

3p\leq 2n-2
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-2},1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+4+\dots+p-4+(p-3)\cdot 2)

\displaystyle\quad-(p-2+p-1+p+p+1)-(p+3+\dots+2n-2p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-2)(p-4)}{4}-\frac{(p-2)^{2}}{2}-3p+3-\frac{(2n-p)(n-\frac{3p}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-1.

(ii)

n=p+1
and
p>2
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,2,\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n-p-6},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p-2n+6}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3+\dots+2n-p-5)

\displaystyle\quad-(2n-p-3+2n-p-1+\dots+p+1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-1-\frac{(2n-p-4)(2n-p-5)}{2}-\frac{(2n-2)(p-n+3)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+5n-\frac{7}{2}p-np-8

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p-3=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-3.

(iii)

3p\geq 2n
and
n\neq p+1
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-2},\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n-3p+2},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{3p-2n+2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+4+\dots+p-4+(p-3)\cdot 2)

\displaystyle\quad-(p-2+p-1+\dots+2n-2p-1)

\displaystyle\quad-(2n-2p+1+2n-2p+3\dots+p+2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-2)(p-4)}{4}-\frac{(p-2)^{2}}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-3)(2n-3p+2)}{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(2n-p+3)(\frac{3}{2}p-n+\frac{3}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{3p}{2}+n-\frac{13}{4}<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

To sum up,
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
is a reducible point.

(d)

When
z=-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}).

Also we divide the discussion into two cases:

(i)

3p\geq 2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p},\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+4+\dots+2n-2p-2+(2n-2p-1)\cdot 2)

\displaystyle\quad-(2n-2p+2n-2p+1+\dots+p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-2\cdot\frac{(n-p)(n-p-1)}{2}-2\cdot\frac{(2n-2p)(n-p)}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-1)(3p-2n)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

(ii)

3p<2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+4+\dots+p-2+(p-1)\cdot 2)

\displaystyle\quad-(p+1+p+3+\dots+2n-2p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{p(p-2)}{4}-\frac{p^{2}}{2}-\frac{(2n-p)(n-\frac{3p}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

To sum up,
z=-n+\frac{p}{2}
is an irreducible point.

So
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first reducible point.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,
we can easily get that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, we have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2p-1)-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-p^{2}-n^{2}+2np-p^{2}

(5.6)

\displaystyle=2np-2p^{2}<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=-\frac{3}{2}
, by (
7.7
) and (
7.8
),
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,\frac{p}{2}-2,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-1),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{p-3},1,0,1,0,1,0).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+3+\dots+p-3+p-1+p+1)-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-2)(p-3)}{2}-2p-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-3=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-3.

So
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
is a reducible point.

(d)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{p},1,0).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+3+\dots+p-2+p-1)-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}-(n-p)^{2}

(5.7)

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first reducible point.

All in all, when
p
is even,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(1-n+\frac{p}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=1-n+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Finally we consider the case when
p
is odd and
3p<2n+1
.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
.

(2)

When
z=-1
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-1
.

(3)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}).

Since
p
is odd, we have the follows.

(a)

When
3p=2n-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},1,1,1,1,0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+\dots+p-5+(p-4)\cdot 2)-(p-3+\dots+p+p+2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-3)(p-5)}{4}-\frac{(p-3)^{2}}{2}-5p+4

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{3}{4}p^{2}-\frac{17}{4}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-4=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-4.

(b)

When
3p<2n-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},1,1,1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-3}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+\dots+p-5+(p-4)\cdot 2)-(p-3+\dots+p+1+p+2)

\displaystyle-(p+4+p+6+\dots+2n-2p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-3)(p-5)}{4}-\frac{(p-3)^{2}}{2}-5p+5-\frac{(2n-p+1)(n-\frac{3}{2}p-\frac{1}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-3=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-3.

Hence when
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
will be reducible.

(4)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}).

Since
p
is odd, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-1},1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-1}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(2+2+3\cdot 2+\dots+p-3+(p-2)\cdot 2+p-1)

\displaystyle-(p+p+2+p+4+\dots+2n-2p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-1)(p+1)}{4}-\frac{(p-1)^{2}}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-1)(n-\frac{3}{2}p+\frac{1}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So when
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(5)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
and
z\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, by(
3a
) we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}
.

(6)

When
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{3}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{p-2},1,0,1,0).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+3+\dots+p-3+p-2+p)-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)}{2}-(n-p)^{2}-p

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-1.

Hence when
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(7)

When
z=-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+\dots+p-1)-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So when
z=-n+\frac{p}{2}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

In other words,
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
and
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
are the first two reducible points, and in this case,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(1-n+\frac{p}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=1-n+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
by Lemma
2.2
.

So far, we have completed the proof of all cases of the second case of type
B_{n}
.
∎

Example 5.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(7,\mathbb{C})
. We consider the parabolic subalgebra
\mathfrak{q}
corresponding to the subset
I=\Pi\setminus\{\alpha_{3}\}
. In this case, we have
p=3
and
n=3
. So the scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(z\xi_{3})
is reducible if and only if

z\in 1-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}=-2+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. This result is the same with
[
10
]
and
[
16
]
.

The arguments of
\mathfrak{sp}(2n,\mathbb{C})
are similar to that of
\mathfrak{so}(2n+1,\mathbb{C})
. Next, we will discuss the reducibility of the scalar generalized Verma modules of
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}(2n,\mathbb{C})
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}(2n,\mathbb{C})
. The scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if

(1)

if
p=\mathrm{1}
, then

z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0};

(2)

if
\mathrm{2}\leq p\leq n-1
, then

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\text{if $p$ is odd or $3p>2n$}\\
\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $p$ is even and\>\>$3p\leq 2n$};\\
\end{array}\right.

(3)

if
p=n
, then

z\in\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

First, let’s consider the case
p=1
.

Take
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{n}\}
, where
\alpha_{i}=e_{i}-e_{i+1}\>\>1\leq i\leq n-1
and
\alpha_{n}=2e_{n}
. When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type,
\lambda=z\xi
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.1
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute
that
\xi=(1,0,\dots,0)
,
so

\lambda+\rho=(z+n,n-1,n-1,\dots,1),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n,\dots,1,-1,\dots,1-n,-z-n).

Then we have the follows.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
,

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}
is an integral weight.

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n})\>\>{\rm and}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}=(\underbrace{0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n}).

By Lemma
3.7
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 0+(2n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-1,n-1,\dots,1,-1,\dots,1-n,1-n).

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -59.06 M 0 -59.06 L 0 -88.58 L 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -59.06 Z M 29.53 -88.58 M 14.76 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.66978pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -118.11 M 0 -118.11 L 0 -147.64 L 29.53 -147.64 L 29.53 -118.11 Z M 29.53 -147.64 M 14.76 -132.87}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-97.63916pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

{\rm So}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2,2,\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n-4})\>\>{\rm and}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,0,1,\dots,0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(2)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n,\dots,1,-1,\dots,1-n,-z-n),

(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(n-1,\dots,1,-1,\dots,1-n)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-}=(z+n,-z-n).

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(n-1,\dots,1,-1,\dots,1-n),

((\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{2n-2})\>\>{\rm and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(z)}=(z+n)\in[\lambda]_{3}.

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1

\displaystyle=2n-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

In summary, when
p=1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Now we consider the case
p=n
.

Take
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n-1}\}
,
where
\alpha_{i}=e_{i}-e_{i+1}\>\>1\leq i\leq n-1
and
\alpha_{n}=2e_{n}
. When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type,
\lambda=z\xi
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.1
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute
that
\xi=(1,1,\dots,1)
, then

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n,z+n-1,\dots,z+1,-z-1,\dots,-z+1-n,-z-n).

First we consider the case when
p=n
is even.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
,

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}
is an integral weight, so

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1).

By Lemma
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 0+(2n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-1,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,1-n).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2,\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1)}_{2n-2}\>\>{\rm and}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,0,\dots,1,0)}_{2n-1}.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-3)\cdot 1+(2n-2)\cdot 0)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2n-3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=2n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

z=1-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2}+1,\frac{n}{2},\dots,-\frac{n}{2}+2,\frac{n}{2}-2,\dots,-\frac{n}{2},-\frac{n}{2}-1).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-3},1,0,1,0,1,0).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

(5.10)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-3)\cdot 1+(n-1)\cdot 1+(n+1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+\dots+n-3)-2n

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n-2)(n-3)}{2}-2n

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2},\frac{n}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{n}{2}+1,\frac{n}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{n}{2}+1,-\frac{n}{2}).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-118.97873pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n)(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=1-\frac{n}{2}
is the first reducible point.

(2)

If
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we will have
(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(0)
and
(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-}=(0)
, then

\lambda+\rho=(z+n,z+n-1,\dots,z+1)\in[\lambda]_{3}.

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{x\in[\lambda]_{3}}F_{A}(\tilde{x})

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{n(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

If
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n,z+n-1,\dots,z+1,-z-1,\dots,-z+1-n,-z-n),

(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}^{-}=(0)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-}=(\lambda+\rho)^{-}.

(a)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,0,\dots,1,0}_{2n}).

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)_{(\frac{1}{2})}^{-})^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 0+\dots+2(n-1)\cdot 1+(2n-1)\cdot 0)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-2\cdot\frac{n(n-1)}{2}=n<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=-\frac{3}{2}
, we will have

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,0,1,0\dots,1,0}_{2n-2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 0+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 1+(2n-3)\cdot 0)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+4+\dots+2n-4)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-1-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=3n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{n}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-\frac{n}{2},\frac{n}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2},-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}).

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n-2},1,0,1,0).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(n-3)\cdot 1+(n-2)\cdot 1+(n-1)\cdot 0+n\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+\dots+n-2)-n

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}-n

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(d)

When
z=-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{n}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2},\frac{n}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-\frac{n}{2},\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}).

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -59.06 M 29.53 -59.06 L 29.53 -88.58 L 59.06 -88.58 L 59.06 -59.06 Z M 59.06 -88.58 M 44.29 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{32.00935pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -88.58 M 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -118.11 L 59.06 -118.11 L 59.06 -88.58 Z M 59.06 -118.11 M 44.29 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{23.90065pt}{-75.93849pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{n}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(n-3)\cdot 1+(n-2)\cdot 1+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+\dots+n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{n(n-1)}{2}=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible. And
z=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}
is the first reducible point when
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
.

When
p=n
is odd, the argument is similar.

In summary, for the case
p=n
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in\{\frac{1-n}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}\cup\{1-\frac{n}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Finally, let’s consider the case
2\leq p\leq n-1
. Under the circumstances, the arguments of type
B_{n}
and type
C_{n}
are almost the same, so we will omit the proof of this part.
∎

6.
Reducibility of scalar genralized Verma modules for type
D_{n}

In this section, we consider the type
D_{n}
. Let
M_{I}(\lambda)
be a scalar generalized Verma module with highest weight
\lambda=z\xi
, where
\xi=\xi_{p}
is the fundamental weight corresponding to the simple root
\alpha_{p}
. We have the following proposition.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n,\mathbb{C})
.
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if

(1)

if
p=\mathrm{1}
, then

\quad z\in 2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
;

(2)

if
\mathrm{2}\leq p\leq n-2
, then

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{3}{2}+\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $p$ is odd or}\>\>3p<2n\\
1+\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $p$ is even and}\>\>3p\geq 2n;\end{array}\right.

(3)

if
p=n-1
or
n
, then

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $n$ is even}\\
3-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\textnormal{if $n$ is odd.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

For
p=1
, we take
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{n}\}
, where
\alpha_{i}=e_{i}-e_{i+1}\>\>1\leq i\leq n-1
and
\alpha_{n}=e_{n-1}+e_{n+1}
. When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type, we know that
\lambda=z\xi
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.1
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute
that
\xi=(1,0,\dots,0)
, (see details in
[
15
]
),
so

\lambda+\rho=(z+n-1,n-2,\dots,0),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n-1,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,1-n-z).

Then we have the follows.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
,
\lambda+\rho
is integral.

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(1,\underbrace{0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-2}).

By Lemma
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{n(n-1)}{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-2,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,2-n).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-6}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-5)\cdot 0+(2n-4)\cdot 1

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-1-2(1+2+\dots+n-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-1-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=2-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(1,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,-1).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-6}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-5)\cdot 0+(2n-4)\cdot 1

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-1-2(1+2+\dots+n-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-1-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=1-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(0,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,0).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -59.06 M 0 -59.06 L 0 -88.58 L 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -59.06 Z M 29.53 -88.58 M 14.76 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.66978pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{0,1,0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-4}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 2+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-5)\cdot 0+(2n-4)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-2=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=2-n
is the first reducible point when
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{ev}=(1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-4})\>\>\text{and}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(1).

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-5)\cdot 0+(2n-4)\cdot 1)-(0\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-2=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{ev}=(1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-4})\>\>\text{and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)_{(z)}=(z+n-1)\in[\lambda]_{3}.

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-5)\cdot 0+(2n-4)\cdot 1)-(0\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-2=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Combining (2) and (3), we can get a conclusion that if
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

In summary, when
p=1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in 2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Now we consider the case
p=n-1
.

Take
\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{l})=\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n-2},\alpha_{n}\}
,
where
\alpha_{i}=e_{i}-e_{i+1}\>\>1\leq i\leq n-1
and
\alpha_{n}=e_{n-1}+e_{n+1}
. When
M_{I}(\lambda)
is of scalar type,
\lambda=z\xi
for some
z\in\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.1
.

By the definition of fundamental weight, we can compute
that
\xi=(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2})
, (see details in
[
15
]
), then

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}z+n-1,\dots,\frac{1}{2}z,-\frac{1}{2}z,\dots,1-n-\frac{1}{2}z).

First we consider the case when
n
is even.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>0
,
\lambda+\rho
is integral and
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,0,\dots,1,0)
.

By Lemma
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{n(n-1)}{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=0
, we will have

(6.2)

\displaystyle(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-1,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,1-n),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-2}).

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{n(n-1)}{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=2-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-1,\dots,1-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-1,\dots,1-\frac{1}{2}n,-\frac{1}{2}n).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-118.97873pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\underbrace{2,2,\dots,2}_{n-1},1,1)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-1},0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+\dots+(n-2)\cdot 1+(n-1)\cdot 0+n\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+2+\dots+n-2)-n

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}-n

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n-1<\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=1-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n,\dots,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n).

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{32.00935pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -88.58 M 0 -88.58 L 0 -118.11 L 29.53 -118.11 L 29.53 -88.58 Z M 29.53 -118.11 M 14.76 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
