{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -88.58 M 29.53 -88.58 L 29.53 -118.11 L 59.06 -118.11 L 59.06 -88.58 Z M 59.06 -118.11 M 44.29 -103.35}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{23.90065pt}{-75.93849pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,1,\dots,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{ev}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+2+\dots+n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{n(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Thus
z=2-n
is the first reducible point when
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

If
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}z+n-1,\dots,\frac{1}{2}z,-\frac{1}{2}z,\dots,1-n-\frac{1}{2}z),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{ev}=(0)\>\>\text{and}\>\>(\lambda+\rho)=(\frac{1}{2}z+n-1,\dots,\frac{1}{2}z)\in[\lambda]_{3}.

By Theorem
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(n-1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+2+\dots+n-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{n(n-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible by Lemma
3.11
.

To sum up, when
n
is even,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in 2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

When
n
is odd, the arguments are similar to the case when
n
is even. Finally, we can get the result that
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in 3-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We omit the details here.

All in all, when
p=n-1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if

z\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\text{if $n$ is even}\\
3-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\text{if $n$ is odd}.\end{array}\right.

The arguments of the case when
p=n
are similar to that of
p=n-1
, we omit the details here.

Finally we consider the case when
2\leq p\leq n-2
.

First we consider the case when p is even and
3p\geq 2n
.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n-1,\dots,z+n-p,n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\dots,-z-n+1),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-2},1).

By Theorem
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 0+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-2)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2(1+2+\dots+n-1)

(6.3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{n(n-1)}{2}=0<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(n-2,\dots,n-p-1,n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\dots,-n+2).

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

In the above Young tableau,
k=2n-p
.

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-6},1).

By Theorem
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-1-2(1+2+\dots+n-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-1-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p-2},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n},0,1).

By Theorem
3.7
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 2+\dots+(2n-2p-2)\cdot 2)-(2n-2p-1+\dots+p-2)-p

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p-1)^{2}-4\cdot\frac{(n-p)(n-p-1)}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-3)(3p-2n)}{2}-p

(6.4)

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(c)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2},n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p-2},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n+1}).

By Theorem
3.7
and (
1b
), we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p-1)^{2}-4\cdot C^{2}_{n-p}-(2n-2p-1+\dots+p-2)-p+1

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1
is the first reducible point when
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

If
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1)\text{~and~}(\lambda+\rho)_{(z)}=(z+n-1,\dots,z+n-p)\in[\lambda]_{3}.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+\dots+p-1)-(2+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}-2\cdot\frac{(n-p)(n-p-1)}{2}

(6.5)

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

So
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

If
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
and
z\geq p-n
, we have

(6.6)

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{ev}=(1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-2p-2})\text{~and~}p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,0,\dots,1,0}_{2p}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+\dots+2p-2)-(2+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-2\cdot\frac{p(p-1)}{2}-2\cdot\frac{(n-p)(n-p-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-C^{2}_{p}<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(a)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1+\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{3}{2},n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}).

\tiny{\hbox to43.08pt{\vbox to85.76pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-85.55827pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{p-2},1,0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-(1+2+\dots+p-2+p)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)}{2}-p

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}).

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{24.25337pt}{-75.86906pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So

(6.7)

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{p}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-(1+2+\dots+p-2+p-1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)}{2}-p+1

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Hence,
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1+\frac{1}{2}
is the first reducible point when
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
.

All in all, when
p
is even and
3p\geq 2n
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(\frac{p}{2}-n+1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\frac{p}{2}-n+1+\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=\frac{p}{2}-n+1+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Next, let’s look at the case when
p
is odd.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
,

M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible by (
1
).

(a)

When
z=-1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible by (
1b
).

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}).

We divide the discussion into three cases:

(i)

p
is odd and
3p>2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p-2},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n-1},1,0,1).

By (
1b
),
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(ii)

p
is odd and
2n-5<3p<2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{2n-3p+1},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{3p-2n+3}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+4+\dots+p-4+(p-3)\cdot 2)-(p-2+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle\quad-(2n-2p+2n-2p+2\dots+p+1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(p-3)\frac{p-3}{2}}{2}-4\cdot\frac{\frac{p-1}{2}\cdot\frac{p-3}{2}}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-4)(2n-3p+1)}{2}

\displaystyle\quad-(n-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2})(\frac{3p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2})

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-2p+n-\frac{5}{2}<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(iii)

p
is odd and
3p\leq 2n-5
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-3}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+4+\dots+p-4+(p-3)\cdot 2)-(p-2+p-1+p)

\displaystyle\quad-(p+1+p+3+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(p-3)\frac{p-3}{2}}{2}-4\cdot\frac{\frac{p-1}{2}\cdot\frac{p-3}{2}}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-1)(n-\frac{3}{2}p-\frac{1}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

Combining with (i),(ii) and (iii),
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
is a reducible point.

(c)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}).

We divide the discussion into two cases:

(i)

p
is odd and
3p+1\geq 2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p-2},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n+1}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+4+\dots+(2n-2p-2)\cdot 2)-(2n-2p-1+\dots+p-2)-p+1

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(ii)

p
is odd and
3p+1<2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-1},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-1}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+4+\dots+p-2+(p-1)\cdot 2)-(p+1+p+3+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(p-1)\frac{p-1}{2}}{2}-4\cdot\frac{\frac{p+1}{2}\cdot\frac{p-1}{2}}{2}-\frac{(2n-p-1)(n-\frac{3}{2}p-\frac{1}{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

From (i) and (ii), we can see that
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}
is an irreducible point.

So
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
is the first reducible point when
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

If
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,

M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible by (
2
).

(3)

If
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
and
z\geq p-n
,

M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible by (
6.6
).

(a)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,\frac{p}{2}-2,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-1).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{p-3},1,0,1,0,1,0).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-(1+2+\dots+p-2+p)-2

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)}{2}-p-2

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{32.00935pt}{-32.00935pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.66978pt}{-53.34892pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 29.53 -59.06 M 29.53 -59.06 L 29.53 -88.58 L 59.06 -88.58 L 59.06 -59.06 Z M 59.06 -88.58 M 44.29 -73.82}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{24.25337pt}{-54.5295pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{p-1},1).

By (
6.7
),
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
is the first reducible point when
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
.

To sum up, when
p
is odd,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\frac{p}{2}-n+2+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Ultimately, we discuss the case when
p
is even and
3p<2n
.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
z>-1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible by (
1
).

(a)

When
z=-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p-6}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=2n-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-2,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-1).

Since
p
is even, we have the follows.

(i)

p=2
and
3p\geq 2n-4
. The the corresponding Young tableau has only two columns. So it is easy to calculate that
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)<\dim(\mathfrak{u})
.

(ii)

p=2
and
3p<2n-4
. Similar to the above case.

(iii)

p>2
and
3p\geq 2n-4
. We have

(6.8)

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-4},1,1,1,1,0,1)

(6.9)

\displaystyle{\rm or~~}

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-4},1,1,1,1,1,1).

For (
6.8
), we get

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 2+\dots+(p-4)\cdot 2)-(p-3+\dots+p+p+2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(p-4)\cdot\frac{p-4}{2}}{2}-4\cdot\frac{\frac{p-2}{2}\cdot\frac{p-4}{2}}{2}-5p+4

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{3}{4}p^{2}-4<\dim(\mathfrak{u})\quad({\rm since}\>\>3p=2n-2\text{~or~}2n-4).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

For (
6.9
), we get

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-n-\frac{3}{4}p^{2}-p-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u})\quad({\rm since}\>\>3p=2n-2\text{~or~}2n-4).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(iv)

p>2
and
3p<2n-4
. We have

((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-4},1,1,1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-4}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 2+\dots+(p-4)\cdot 2)-(p-3+\dots+p+p+1)

\displaystyle\quad-(p+2+p+4+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(p-4)\cdot\frac{p-4}{2}}{2}-4\cdot\frac{\frac{p-2}{2}\cdot\frac{p-4}{2}}{2}-5p+5-\frac{(2n-p)(n-\frac{3}{2}p-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-3<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

Hence when
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
will be reducible.

(c)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-2},1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 2+\dots+(p-4)\cdot 2)-(p-1+p)

\displaystyle\quad-(p+2+p+4+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{(p-2)\cdot\frac{p-2}{2}}{2}-4\cdot\frac{\frac{p}{2}\cdot\frac{p-2}{2}}{2}-2p+1-\frac{(2n-p)(n-\frac{3}{2}p-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

So far we have proved that
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
is the first reducible point when
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and
3p<2n
.

(2)

If
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,

M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible by (
2
).

(3)

If
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
and
z\geq p-n
,

M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible by (
6.6
).

(a)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
, we will have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}).

\tiny{\hbox to21.74pt{\vbox to85.76pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-85.55827pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 -29.53 L 29.53 -29.53 L 29.53 0 Z M 29.53 -29.53 M 14.76 -14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.82051pt}{-11.26007pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -29.53 M 0 -29.53 L 0 -59.06 L 29.53 -59.06 L 29.53 -29.53 Z M 29.53 -59.06 M 14.76 -44.29}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.96837pt}{-76.29959pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=P(\lambda).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{p-1},0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-(1+2+\dots+p-2+p)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(n-p)^{2}+n-p-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)}{2}-p

\displaystyle=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

By Lemma
3.11
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible.

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,1,1,\dots,1}_{p}).

By (
6.7
),
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

Hence,
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
is the first reducible point when
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
and
3p<2n
.

All in all, when
3p<2n
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is reducible if and only if
z\in(\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\cup(\frac{p}{2}-n+2+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Finally we completed the proof of our proposition.

∎

7.
Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions of scalar generalized verma modules

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a finite-dimensional complex semisimple Lie
algebra. For a maximal parabolic subalgebra
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{u}
, if
\mathfrak{u}
is abelian, the corresponding scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(\lambda)
will be called
Hermitian type
. These modules are very important in the calssification of unitary highest weight moudles, see for example

[
7
,
9
]
.

We use
L(z\xi_{p})
to denote the irreducible quotient of the scalar generalized Verma module
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
.
In this section, we will give the specific Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions of highest weight moudles
L(z\xi_{p})
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{C})
. Denote
r=\min\{p,n-p\}
. Then

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}p(n-p)&\textnormal{if}\>z<1-r\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(n-k)&\textnormal{if}\>z=-k\in\mathbb{Z},1\leq k\leq r-1\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

Proof.

This is a direct corollary of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

From
[
8
]
, we know that the highest weight module
L(-k\xi_{p})
is the
k
-th Wallach representation of
SU(p,q)
for
1\leq k\leq r-1
, which is unitary.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n+1,\mathbb{C})
. Then we have

(1)

If
p=1
, then

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2n-1&\textnormal{if}\>z<\frac{3}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\in\mathbb{Z}_{<0}\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2n-2&\textnormal{if}\>z=\frac{3}{2}-n+k,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(2)

If
2\leq p\leq n-1
, then

(a)

When
3p\geq 2n+1
and
p
is odd, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}&\textnormal{if~}z<\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}\textnormal{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,1\leq k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,1\leq k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,n-p<k\leq\frac{p-1}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k-\frac{1}{2},k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+2p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}.\end{array}\right.

(b)

When
p
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<1-n+\frac{p}{2}\textnormal{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p\geq k\emph{~and~}1\leq k\leq n-p\\
p(2n-2p+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p<k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,k<\frac{p}{2}\emph{~and~}1\leq k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,n-p<k\leq\frac{p}{2}-1\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k-\frac{1}{2},k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+2p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-4}{2}.\par\par\par\end{array}\right.

(c)

When
3p<2n+1
and
p
is odd, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}&\textnormal{if~}z<1-n+\frac{p}{2}\textnormal{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p\geq k\emph{~and~}1\leq k\leq n-p\\
p(2n-2p+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p<k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,1\leq k\leq 2p-n\\
2np-2p^{2}+(2k+1)(p-k)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,2p-n<k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k-\frac{1}{2},k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+2p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}.\par\par\par\par\par\par\par\par\end{array}\right.

(3)

If
p=n
, then

(a)

When
n
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{n})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<2-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\>\>\textnormal{or}\>-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-2}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\end{array}\right.

(b)

When
n
is odd, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{n})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<1-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\>\>\textnormal{or}\>-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-1}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Some details of the arguments can be found in the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
First we consider the case
p=1
.

(1)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, we know that
z=0
is the first reducible point by Proposition
5.1
. And

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{1})=0
when
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{1})=\dim(\mathfrak{u})=2n-1
when
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{<0}
.

(2)

If
z\in\frac{3}{2}-n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have the follows.

(a)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-n
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-2
from the proof the Proposition
5.1
.

(b)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+k\>\>(k\geq 1)
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-n,-z-n+\frac{1}{2}),

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(z+n-\frac{1}{2},-z-n+\frac{1}{2}).

Since
z+n-\frac{1}{2}>-z-n+\frac{1}{2}
, we get

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(0,1)\>\>{\rm and}\>\>\sum_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})})^{odd}=(n-1)^{2}.

So

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(n-1)^{2}-(0\cdot 0+1\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=2n-2.

Thus, we completed the proof of this case.

Now we consider the case
p=n
.

We can get from the previous calculation that

\xi=(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2})
.

(1)

n
is even. From Proposition
5.1
, we know
z=2-n
is the first reducible point for
M_{I}(z\xi_{n})
.

(a)

When
z=2-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}n+\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-2},0,1,0,1).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(n-3)\cdot 1+(n-1)\cdot 1+(n+1)\cdot 1)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+\dots+n-3)-2n

(7.3)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n-2)(n-3)}{2}-2n=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-3.

(b)

When
z=3-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}n+1,\dots,2-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-2,\dots,-1-\frac{1}{2}n),

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-3},1,0,1,0,1,0)

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-2},0,1,0,1).

By (
1a
),
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-3
.

(c)

When
z=4-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{1}{2}n+\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{5}{2},\dots,-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-4},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{8}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+\dots+(n-5)\cdot 1)-4n

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+2+\dots+n-5)-4n

(7.4)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\frac{(n-4)(n-5)}{2}-4n=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-10.

(d)

When
z=5-n
, we will have

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-5},\underbrace{1,0,\dots,1,0}_{10})

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{n-4},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{8}).

By (
1c
),
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-10
.

(e)

when
z=-2
or
-1

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-1
.

So far, we have calculated all the
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)
, it is not difficult to find the law.

(2)

n
is odd. From Proposition
5.1
, we know
z=1-n
is the first reducible point for
M_{I}(z\xi_{n})
.

The process is similar to the case when
n
is even, so we can get that

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<1-n\textnormal{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k+1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\>\>\textnormal{or}\>-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-1}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.\\

Now we consider the case when
p
is odd and
3p\geq 2n+1
.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.1
. We will have the follows.

(a)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}\geq 0
, we will have
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
by Proposition
5.1
.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,n-p-\frac{1}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-4}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(0\cdot 1+1\cdot 1+2\cdot 0+3\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4)\cdot 0+(2n-3)\cdot 1)

(7.5)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n^{2}+2n-1=2n-1<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

(c)

When
z=-2
, we have
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2^{4},1^{2n-8})
.

So

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-8})
.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}(i-1)p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})_{i}^{odd}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1\cdot 1+2\cdot 1+3\cdot 1+5\cdot 1+\dots+(2n-4-1)\cdot 1)

(7.6)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-2-(n-2)^{2}=4n-6<\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

(d)

When
z=-k
,
1\leq k\leq n-p
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2^{2k},1^{2n-4k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2k(n-k+\frac{1}{2})
.

(e)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
1\leq k\leq n-p
, we have

{p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(3^{2k},2^{2n-2p-2k},1^{4p-2k-2n})}.

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+n
.

(f)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
1+n-p\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{2}
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(3^{2n-2p},2^{2k-2n+2p},1^{2p-4k}).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+k
.

(g)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}).

So
p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n-1},0,1).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible.

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
,
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.1
. We will have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-p^{2}-(n-p)^{2}=2np-2p^{2}
.

(b)

When
z=-\frac{3}{2}
, we will have

(7.7)

\displaystyle(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2}),

(7.8)

\displaystyle(\lambda+\rho)_{(0)}=(n-2,\dots,n-p-1,-n+p+1,\dots,-n+2).

We divide the discussion into two cases:

(i)

p=n-1
. We will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})=(2,1^{2p-2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p-2}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+2p-1.

(ii)

p<n-1
. We will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}.

In general, when
z=-k-\frac{1}{2}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}\text{~if~}k<n-p.

And

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})=(2^{2p+2k+1-2n},1^{4n-2p-4k-2})\text{~if }n-p\leq k\leq n-\frac{p+3}{2}.

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+(k-n+p)(2n-2k-3)+2p-1
.
In other words, when
z=-(n-p)-k-\frac{1}{2}
with
0\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+2p-1.

Next we consider the case when
p
is even.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first recucible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.1
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq 0
, we will have
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we have
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-1
since (
1b
).

(c)

When
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we divide the discussion into three cases:

(i)

3p\leq 2n-2
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-2},1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-2}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1.

(ii)

n=p+1
and
p>2
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,2,\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n-p-6},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2p-2n+6}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p-3.

(iii)

3p\geq 2n
and
n\neq p+1
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-2},\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{2n-3p+2},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{3p-2n+2}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{3p}{2}+n-\frac{13}{4}.

(d)

When
z=-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-n+p+\frac{1}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2}).

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

(e)

When
p\geq k
,

z=-k
,
1\leq k\leq n-p
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2^{2k},1^{2n-4k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2k(n-k+\frac{1}{2})
.

(f)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
1\leq k\leq n-p
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(3^{2k},2^{2n-2p-2k},1^{4p-2k-2n}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+n
.

(g)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
1+n-p\leq k\leq\frac{p}{2}-1
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(3^{2n-2p},2^{2k-2n+2p},1^{2p-4k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+2kp-2k^{2}+k
.

(h)

When
p<k
,
z=-k
,
p<k\leq n-p
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})=(2^{2p},1^{2n-4p}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=p(2n-2p+1)
.

(i)

When
z<1-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,

M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible, so we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first recucible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.1
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-(1+3+\dots+2p-1)-(n-p)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}-p^{2}-n^{2}+2np-p^{2}

(7.9)

\displaystyle=2np-2p^{2}.

(b)

In general, when
z=-k-\frac{1}{2}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}\text{~if~}k<n-p.

(c)

When
z=-(n-p)-k-\frac{1}{2}
,
0\leq k\leq\frac{p-4}{2}
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})=(2^{2p+2k+1-2n},1^{4n-2p-4k-2}).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+2p-1
.

Finally we consider the case when
p
is odd and
3p<2n+1
.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first recucible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.1
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-1
.

(c)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

\lambda+\rho=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,n-p-\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{1}{2}).

Since
p
is odd, we have the follows.

(i)

When
3p=2n-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},1,1,1,1,0,1).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-4.

(ii)

When
3p<2n-1
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},1,1,1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-3}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-3.

(d)

In general, when
p\geq k
,

z=-k
,
1\leq k\leq n-p
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2^{2k},1^{2n-2k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=k(2n-2p+1).

(e)

When
z=-k
,
p<k\leq n-p
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2^{2p},1^{2n-2p}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=p(2n-2p+1).

(f)

When
p\geq n-p+k
,

z=-(n-p)-k
,
1\leq k\leq 2p-n
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{2k},2^{2n-2p-2k},1^{4p-2n-2k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}-2k^{2}+2kp+n.

(g)

When
p<n-p+k
,

z=-(n-p)-k
,
2p-n<k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{2k},2^{2p-4k},1^{-4p+2n+2k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}-2k^{2}+2kp+p-k.

(h)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
p-3<2k
, equivalently
z<\frac{p+3}{2}-n
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{p},1^{2n-3p}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

(2)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
,
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
is the first recucible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.1
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, by (
3a
) we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}
.

(b)

When
z=1-n+\frac{p}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{3}{2},\frac{p}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,1,\dots,1}_{p-2},1,0,1,0).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{p}{2}-1=\dim(\mathfrak{u})-1.

(c)

In general, when
z=-k-\frac{1}{2}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}\text{~if~}k<n-p.

(d)

When
z=-(n-p)-k-\frac{1}{2}
,
0\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}
, we have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)})=(2^{2p+2k+1-2n},1^{4n-2p-4k-2}).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+2p-1
.

∎

From
[
8
]
, we know that the highest weight module
L(-(n-\frac{3}{2})\xi_{1})
is the unique Wallach representation of
SO(2,2n-1)
, which is unitary.

For type
B_{n}
, we have given the detailed arguments for the calculations in the case when
2\leq p\leq n-1
. Now we omit some similar arguments for types
C_{n}
and
D_{n}
.

Proposition 7.10
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n,\mathbb{C})
and
L(z\xi_{p})
be a scalar highest weight module. Then we have the follows.

(1)

If
p=1
, then

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2n-2&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<2-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
2n-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>2-n\leq z\leq-1\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(2)

If
2\leq p\leq n-2
, then

(a)

When
3p\geq 2n
and
p
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<1+\frac{p}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2nk-2k^{2}-k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,1\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)+n-p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,0\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,n-p\leq k<\frac{p}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k+\frac{1}{2},k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-4}{2}.\par\par\end{array}\right.

(b)

When
p
is odd, we have

\small{{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<\frac{3+p}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2nk-2k^{2}-k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,k<n-p\emph{~and~}1\leq k\leq p\\
2np-2p^{2}-p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p<k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,\max\{0,2p-n\}<k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)+n-p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,k\leq 2p-n\emph{~and~}0\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,n-p\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k+\frac{1}{2},k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-5}{2}.\\
\end{array}\right.}

(c)

When
3p<2n
and
p
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<\frac{3+p}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2nk-2k^{2}-k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,1\leq k<n-p\emph{~and~}k<p\\
2np-2p^{2}-p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)+n-p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,0\leq k\leq 2p-n\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,2p-n<0\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k+\frac{1}{2},k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-4}{2}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(3)

If
p=n-1\>\>or\>\>p=n
, then

(a)

When
n
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<2-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\emph{~or~}-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-2}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(b)

When
n
is odd, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<3-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\emph{~or~}-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-3}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The details of the arguments for the case
p=1
can be found in the proof of Proposition
5.9
.

Now we consider the case
p=n
. From Proposition
5.9
,
z=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{n})
.

(1)

When
z=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-1
.

(2)

When
z=1-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-3},1,0,1,0,1,0)=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-2},0,1,0,1).

By (
1b
),

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-3
.

(3)

When
z=\frac{3}{2}-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{n}{2},\dots,-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{5}{2},\frac{n}{2}-\frac{5}{2},\dots,-\frac{n}{2}-\frac{3}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-4},1,0,1,0,1,0,1,0)=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-3},0,1,0,1,0,1).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-6.

(4)

When
z=2-\frac{n}{2}
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-5},\underbrace{1,0,\dots,1,0}_{10})=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-4},0,1,0,1,0,1,0,1).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n-10.

(5)

When
z=-2
, we will have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{1,0,\dots,1,0}_{2n-6}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=4n-6.

In general, we find that
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=kn-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}
if
z=-\frac{k}{2}
for
1\leq k\leq n-1
.

When
z=-\frac{1}{2}+k
,
z=k

(k\geq 0)
and
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have obtained
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)
in Proposition
5.9
.

For the case
2\leq p\leq n-1
, we only consider the case when
3p\leq 2n
and
p
is even.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{p}{2}-n
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.9
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z>-1
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-1.

(c)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+1
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,n-p,\dots,1,-1,\dots,-n+p,\frac{p}{2}-2,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-1),

and

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p-1.

(d)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+1,n-p,\dots,1,-1,\dots,-n+p,\frac{p}{2}-1,\dots,-\frac{p}{2}),

and

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

In general, we can have the follows.

(a)

When
z=-k
,
n-p\geq k-1
and
p\geq k>0
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2^{2k-1},1^{2n-4k+2}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=k(2n-2k+1).

(b)

When
z=-k
,
n-p\geq k-1
and
p<k
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2^{2p},1^{2n-4p}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=p(2n-2p+1).

(c)

When
z=-2k
,
n-p\leq 2(k-1)
and
p\geq 2k
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{4k-2n+2p-2},2^{4n-4p-4k+3},1^{2p-4k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n(4k+2p-n)-2(2k+p^{2})+4k(p+1)-3(n-p)-1.

If we write
z=-(n-p)-k\in 2\mathbb{Z}
,
2\leq k\leq 2p-n
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)-5(n-p)-1.

(d)

When
z=-2k-1
,
n-p+1\leq 2k
and
p\geq 2k+1
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{4k-2n+2p},2^{4n-4p-4k+1},1^{2p-4k-2}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=(2k+p)(4n-4k-2p)+(p-1)(4k+1)-n(2n-1).

If we write
z=-(n-p)-k\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}
,
2\leq k\leq 2p-n
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k+2)-(n-p)-1.

(e)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
2p-n<k\leq\frac{1}{2}p
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{2k-2},2^{2p-4k+4},1^{2n-4p+2k-2}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+(2k-1)(p-k)+2k-1=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k+3)-p-1.

(f)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
k\geq\frac{1}{2}p+1
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(3^{p},1^{2n-3p}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(0)}=(n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1)\text{~and~}(\lambda+\rho)_{(z)}=(z+n-1,\dots,z+n-p)\in[\lambda]_{3}.

\displaystyle{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-(1+\dots+p-1)-(2+\dots+2n-2p-2)

\displaystyle=n^{2}-n-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}-2\cdot\frac{(n-p)(n-p-1)}{2}

\displaystyle=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p=\dim(\mathfrak{u}).

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
5.9
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z\geq-\frac{3}{2}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=p(2n-2p+1).

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{1}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{3}{2},n-p,\dots,1,-1,\dots,-n+p,\frac{p}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}).

So

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p-1.

In general, we have the follows.

(a)

When
z=-k-\frac{1}{2}
,
k\leq n-p
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=p(2n-2p+1).

(b)

When
z=-k-\frac{1}{2}
,
k\geq n-p+1
and
n\leq\frac{1}{2}p+k
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}+\frac{1}{2}p.

(c)

When
z=-(n-p)-k-\frac{1}{2}
,
1\leq k\leq\frac{p-2}{2}
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(2^{2k-2n+2p},1^{4n-4k-2p}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+(p-k)(2k+1)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-1)+p.

∎

From
[
8
]
, we know that the highest weight module
L(-\frac{k}{2}\xi_{n})
is the
k
-th Wallach representation of
Sp(n,\mathbb{R})
for
1\leq k\leq n-1
, which is unitary.

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n,\mathbb{C})
and
L(z\xi_{p})
be a scalar highest weight module. Then we have the follows.

(1)

If
p=1
, then

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2n-2&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<2-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
2n-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>2-n\leq z\leq-1\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(2)

If
2\leq p\leq n-2
, then

(a)

When
3p\geq 2n
and
p
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<1+\frac{p}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2nk-2k^{2}-k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,1\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)+n-p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,0\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,n-p\leq k<\frac{p}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k+\frac{1}{2},k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-4}{2}.\par\par\end{array}\right.

(b)

When
p
is odd, we have

\small{{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<\frac{3+p}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2nk-2k^{2}-k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,k<n-p\emph{~and~}1\leq k\leq p\\
2np-2p^{2}-p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p<k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,\max\{0,2p-n\}<k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)+n-p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,k\leq 2p-n\emph{~and~}0\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,n-p\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k+\frac{1}{2},k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-5}{2}.\\
\end{array}\right.}

(c)

When
3p<2n
and
p
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<\frac{3+p}{2}-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\\
2nk-2k^{2}-k&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,1\leq k<n-p\emph{~and~}k<p\\
2np-2p^{2}-p&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k,p\leq k<n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-2)+n-p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,0\leq k\leq 2p-n\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+p-1&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k,2p-n<0\leq k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}\\
2np-2p^{2}&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-k+\frac{1}{2},k\leq n-p\\
2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2},0\leq k\leq\frac{p-4}{2}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(3)

If
p=n-1\>\>or\>\>p=n
, then

(a)

When
n
is even, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<2-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\emph{~or~}-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-2}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

(b)

When
n
is odd, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(z\xi_{p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n&\textnormal{if}\>\>z<3-n\emph{~or~}z\notin\mathbb{Z}\\
k(2n-2k-1)&\textnormal{if}\>\>z=-2k\emph{~or~}-2k+1,1\leq k\leq\frac{n-3}{2}\\
0&\textnormal{if}\>\>z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.\\
\end{array}\right.

Proof.

First we consider the case when
p=1
. From Proposition
6.1
,
M_{I}(\lambda)
is irreducible when
z<2-n
and
z\notin\mathbb{Z}
. So we only consider the case when
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(1)

When
z=2-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(1,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,-1),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-6}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-n-1-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-3.

(2)

When
z=3-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2,n-2,\dots,0,0,\dots,2-n,-2),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{odd}=(1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-6}).

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=n^{2}-n-1-2\cdot\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2}=2n-3.

(3)

When
z=-1
, similarly we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-3
.

(4)

When
z\geq 0
, We can easily get
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
.

Finally, combining with Proposition
6.1
we can get the formula of
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)
when
p=1
.

Now we consider the case when
p=n-1
and
p=n
.

(1)

When
p=n-1
and
n
is even, we have the follows.

(a)

When
z=2-n
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n-1
from the proof of Proposition
6.1
.

(b)

When
z=3-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{3}{2},\dots,-\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{1}{2}),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-2},1,0,1,0).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n-1.

(c)

When
z=4-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2}+1,\dots,2-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-2,\dots,-\frac{1}{2}n-1),

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-3},0,1,0,1,0,1).

So

(7.12)

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n-6.

(d)

When
z=5-n
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{n}{2}+\frac{3}{2},\dots,\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}n,\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{5}{2},\dots,-\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{3}{2}),

\displaystyle p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-4},\underbrace{1,0,\dots,1,0}_{8})

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n-3},0,1,0,1,0,1).

By (
7.12
),
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}n^{2}-\frac{1}{2}n-6
.

(e)

When
z=-2
and
z=-1
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-3
.

(f)

When
z\geq 0
, we will have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0
.

From the above discussion, we can find the formula of
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)
.

(2)

When
p=n-1
and
n
is odd.

The process is similar to the case when
n
is even. We omit this part of the process.

(3)

When
p=n
.

The process is similar to the case when
p=n-1
, whether
n
is odd or even. We omit this part of the process.

If
2\leq p\leq n-2
, we only consider the case when
p
is odd.

(1)

When
z\in\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
by Proposition
6.1
. We have the follows.

(a)

When
z\leq 0
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=0.

(b)

When
z=-1
, we have
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2n-3.

(c)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+\frac{3}{2}
, we will have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{2},n-p-1,\dots,0,0,\dots,-n+p+1,\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2},\dots,-\frac{p}{2}-\frac{1}{2}).

We divide the discussion into three cases:

(i)

3p>2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{2n-2p-2},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{3p-2n-1},1,0,1).

By (
1b
),

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-1.

(ii)

2n-5<3p<2n
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{2n-3p+1},\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{3p-2n+3}).

So
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-2p+n-\frac{5}{2}.

(iii)

3p\leq 2n-5
. We have

p((\lambda+\rho)^{-})^{ev}=(2,\underbrace{1,2,\dots,1,2}_{p-3},1,1,1,1,\underbrace{0,1,\dots,0,1}_{2n-3p-3}).

So

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2pn-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-1.

In general, we can have the follows.

(a)

When
z=-k
,
n-p\geq k+1
and
1\leq k\leq p
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2^{2k+1},1^{2n-4k-2}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=k(2n-2k-1).

(b)

When
z=-k
,
n-p\geq k+1
and
p<k
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(2^{2p+1},1^{2n-4k-2}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=p(2n-2p-1).

(c)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
0\leq k<n-p
and
p\geq n-p+k
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(4,3^{2k},2^{2n-2p-2k-2},1^{4p-2k-2n}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}-2k(k-p+1)+n-p-1.

(d)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
\frac{p-3}{2}\geq k\geq n-p
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(4,3^{2n-2p-2},2^{2k+2p-2n+2},1^{2p-4k-2}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-1).

(e)

When
z=-(n-p)-k
,
\max\{0,2p-n\}<k\leq\frac{p-3}{2}
, we have

p(\lambda+\rho)^{-}=(4,3^{2k},2^{2p-4k-2},1^{2n--4p+2k}),

and
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-3)+p-1.

(2)

When
z\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p.

(3)

When
z\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
,
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
is the first reducible point of
M_{I}(z\xi_{p})
and we have follows.

(a)

When
z\geq p-n
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}.

(b)

When
z=\frac{p}{2}-n+2
, we have

(\lambda+\rho)^{-}_{(\frac{1}{2})}=(\frac{p}{2}+1,\cdots,-\frac{p}{2}+2,\frac{p}{2}-2,\cdots,-\frac{p}{2}-1).

So

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-\frac{3}{2}p^{2}-\frac{1}{2}p-3.

In general, we have the follows.

(a)

When
z=-k+\frac{1}{2}
,
k\leq n-p
, we have

{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}
.

(b)

When
z=p-n-k-\frac{1}{2}
,
0\leq k\leq\frac{p-5}{2}
, we have
{\rm GKdim}\>L(\lambda)=2np-2p^{2}+k(2p-2k-5)+2p-3
.

∎

From
[
8
]
, we know that the highest weight module
L(-(n-2)\xi_{1})
is the unique Wallach representation of
SO(2,2n-2)
, which is unitary.
L(-2k\xi_{n})
is the
k
-th Wallach representation of
SO^{*}(2n)
for
1\leq k\leq[\frac{n}{2}]-1
, which is unitary.

The Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions of Wallach representations are also uniformly listed in
[
2
, Proposition 4.1]
.
